ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e2040	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e2055	Deputies! Yesterday, in the context of a urgent question, we discussed in great detail this subject of immeasurable suffering, which was added to the displaced people after 1945 in various parts of Europe, for example in Czechoslovakia at the time or in the area of the former Socialist Republic of Yugoslavia. I have said that by means of a bundle of measures at European level, by supporting the accession efforts of these countries, we are trying to achieve a Europeanisation of the legal system, which is then also the right to the European Court of Justice or the ratification of relevant international agreements – which has now occurred in the case of the Czech Republic; in the case of Slovenia it is still not possible – to bring the case to the European Court of Human Rights. The pre-accession strategy is also intended to adapt national rights. However, this also means – and this must be said openly – that it is difficult to address these direct decrees directly in the negotiations – in any case, they have not interrupted continuity. That is why, at every bilateral meeting with the Czech and Slovenian Foreign Ministers, I have done my utmost to address these questions. Clearly there must also be a process of reprocessing in the history of these countries, and gratefully there is also movement there. I should like to remind you, as I did yesterday, that, for example, the Czech President Václav Havel has found very clear words to say that this was absolutely immoral at the time. This kind of process of history will soon or for a long time cause these decrees to disappear from the rule of law. That's my hope, and I'm going to work on it.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e3529	Foreign Minister! Please give us an overview: 221/M What has Austria achieved in the past three months in the EU?
ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e3557	Mr. Vice-Chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e3583	Deputies! We now have about half the time of our presidency. I believe that the work programme of the Austrians has arrived very well and has also met with a very good echo internationally and constructive cooperation. In the meantime, we have been able to achieve some concrete successes. I should like to refer only once again to Kosovo, where the humanitarian assistance which has been built up within a few months now works relatively well, but, of course, the political solution is not yet there. Second area: We have managed to ensure that very good cooperation - the Committee on Budgets, the Council of Foreign Ministers and the COREPER, in other words ambassador cooperation - has put the budgetary guidelines out of dispute, especially for NGOs. This is a subject that could not be solved for years. We've got the approval of all of them, 10th. In November, the accession negotiations began. We have made the issue of children's rights and the fight against child pornography a European priority. On Monday it was decided on its own action programme, and at the UN General Assembly we took this issue as the starting point for moving forward two international conventions. We are working intensively on an environmental rail in the enlargement strategy. On Monday it was decided that this issue, the environment and nuclear safety, will be the central theme of the accession efforts. The Minister for the Environment has established that we now have an agreement on six-litre car with industry. The Agriculture Ministers are working very hard on a reasonable bioscrit directive and initiative. The Minister for Transport, I believe, is just a few weeks before a breakthrough in the mediation to the Fifth Framework Programme for Research and Development, which would be a very big step for Europe as a whole. In the field of human rights, through persistent pressure and through our dialogue with China, we have completed that the two major international conventions have been signed by China this week, the second Convention being ratified this year. These are, I believe, significant progress, and I do not doubt that some very difficult issues are still ahead of us: The solution to the Russia crisis, which cannot come from us alone; the question of how we can make the international financial institutions a powerful instrument – and this is still open –; the question of Kosovo must be resolved politically; and of course the major issue of the agenda is still ahead. A great success, which cannot be estimated at all enough, is the probation test that is currently going through the euro area. Only the current unrest in financial markets and security within Euroland shows how correct and strategic this decision has been in May and how right and good these institutions are already working during the Austrian Presidency.

I am very pleased with this government template and thank you for coming to the state of this government template primarily for our Mrs. Federal Minister, who has always been personally involved, as well as for the officials of the Ministry – primarily Mr. Ministerial Councillor Dr. Aigner, Dr. Kierein and Mrs. Dr. Schopper, who have passed this law in a simple and easy-to-read form. I would also like to thank Dr. Schwarz and Mag. Pazourek, who with much patience were always trying for agreement. I would also like to thank the President of the Austrian Medical Chamber, who immediately renegotiated immediately after his recovery and was thus involved in the state of this government proposal. About the history of this reform: For the first time in 1979, a structural reform of the medical chamber was considered in the Medical Chamber. For about ten years there have been preparatory work on this subject. The accession of the EU meant creating new professional regulations for dentists, redefining the right to discipline, adapting the obligation of confidentiality and advertising, and wishing to reform the chamber. The logical consequence: a new medical law. The most important innovations of this law: The Curias for established and employed doctors and dentists enable a better representation of the doctors. The employees felt little represented by the chamber so far. Through the mismanagement of previous chambers in Vienna, they must make very high payments to the welfare fund. Responsible for this are those officials who protest loud and with inaccurate arguments against the new medical law. The Curias now have clearly defined tasks, and depending on the amount of tasks it is necessary to define the chamber envelopes. One of the most difficult points in the negotiations was collective contract law. My group never doubted that this is reserved for the trade union. Only in this way can conflicts of interest be ruled out between employees and the disadvantaged. If only partial interests are represented for employees, the service provider has the trump card. Even the ÖAAB Group in the Federal Labour Chamber requested that the Medical Chamber not grant competence in collective contractual negotiations. The social-democratic trade unions were able to agree much faster with the doctor's chamber. Until the last minute, President Dr. Neumann had to reach agreement with the Public Service Trade Union. To open wishes and facts of the law: My wish would be to require the training assistants and not to give priority to the university clinic in relation to training facilities. I would have reserved the demonstration of complementary or alternative medical cures, as planned for four weeks, exclusively for universities which do not do so on the basis of profit. The service provider must inform young doctors of their application to the doctor's board; some did not know about the obligation to report and thus suffered damage. An important regulation concerns the provision of information and documentation, as well as the computer-based storage of long-term patient documents. The automation-supported transmission of patient data is now legally secured. If ordination holders die or give up ordinations, it must be ensured that patient cards containing irrevocable data are kept safe for many years. The preparation of fees for private medical activities, already adopted by the Chambers of State Doctors, is now subject to legal coverage. Now to the controversial pensioners: They may continue to be active in the family association and as an expert. In the case of regular professional training and payment of the chamber and the welfare fund contribution, they are ordinary members of the board. This corresponds to the use of the other chambers. It was not until 1989 that the resident doctor was introduced. Since the doctors were once deprived of the doctor’s ID and were not allowed to treat them even again in the family circle, they were given proper membership by the creation of the resident doctor, but this was not intended in itself. It was intended to enable pensioners to continue to be doctors and no longer to pass the doctor's card. The maximum number of board members should be increased accordingly to 100 if necessary, and the Board of Directors will be up to 25. The desire to allocate mandates according to the electoral participation – demanded by small groups that exert very strong group pressure – was not included as constitutionally dubious in the government template. The death aid is no longer reserved exclusively for widows and orphans, but also for legal heirs. As a new task, the Austrian Medical Chamber receives the review of laws and regulations. A wish of the Vienna Medical Chamber was to be represented in the Austrian Medical Chamber according to its strength. The Austrian Medical Chamber as a negotiating partner of the Ministry did not agree. In the future, the mandatory education committee in the Austrian Chamber of Physicians for Medical Education and Training is the key to the future. In a short time, we start with exams for general doctors and specialists. For the contested notification obligation: During my studies, children have never heard or learned about sexual abuse or violence in childhood medicine, gynecology, psychiatry or judicial medicine. That's an absolute taboo. Events 1994 and 1995 in Vienna were moderately visited or failed in lack of interest. When it comes to diagnostic insecurity and to the welfare and protection of families, it is far more important to notify youth welfare than to do nothing because of fear of depriving innocent people. The possibility of ad has never been taken by the doctors. They have the possibility that they just don't have the indispensable obligation anymore. In the case of disabled persons or dementia, the suspicion of neglect or abuse shall be reported to the court of appeal. The term of office of the medical board ends with an annual period, and the reform of the welfare fund is therefore carried out for legal reasons at a later date. I am convinced that the proven teams will succeed in a short time. Opposition politicians reject this law because they must always be against everything contained in a government template. But I have shown you here how much positive and how much innovation this law brings. I recognize this law, which contains substantial improvements, which I have just described to you in terms of fractional terms. I would like to thank once again all those who have been exemplary in this government template, and I am very glad that we will finally decide today on the medical reform which many doctors have wanted and longed for and which is very necessary, the Medical Law 1998!

That's a problem. We may be there in Austria on an island of the Blessed. I don't think so. This island of the Blessed could soon come to waving. That is why we need highly motivated doctors who, together with politics, are trying to keep the high level for us. I think it can only go together. I therefore criticise, for example, that in health policy we often argue with keywords, for example with a term such as "spitals deficit". That's a total nonsense! No human is talking about school and federal defiance. We often ask subtly: What do we need that for? – This is the haughtiness of those who are healthy – I have never heard of anything like this before. We also like to talk about the need for greater efficiency. If you take 15 percent more heart surgery in a year alone – how do you want to be efficient? Do you want to operate in a shorter period of time, or do you want to put the patients on the intensive care centre for a shorter period of time? I do not think it can be the aim of a health policy that, as in America, one is sent home with dripping breast cancer wound. I do not want to, and that is not what the ÖVP wants! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] But what I don't want to do is to train in the flash blue any doctors who don't need a human being, train them badly, and then tell them: Bad luck! I have now heard from a leading trade unionist that electricians are also unemployed. This makes it too easy, because there is three times as high unemployment as average unemployment in the medical community. That alone should be an argument. But what does it make sense to have doctors wait at least three years after their studies? Is that good for the patient? We all may be patients once. Does it make sense for doctors, after completing their training, to wait ten years and longer for any employment? I think we need to divide the work better. In my opinion, fewer doctors are probably better for the whole system. This dialogue with the doctors – and this doctor's law gives us the opportunity to conduct the dialogue with the doctors, as they can now better organize themselves and better democratically adapt to each other – we must lead in good style, because it is ultimately about good solutions for the patients. This medical law will motivate many doctors to participate and not to emigrate. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e7151	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Mr President of the Chamber of Physicians! Ladies and gentlemen! The essence of democracy is, in fact, freedom of expression and in decisions, the principle of the majority, and I do not know why this principle is simply doubted or denied by this majority principle. [[towards the liberals]] It is quite clear that there are other opinions. [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] There are also different opinions among doctors, of course. Or don't you have any other opinions? Is there among you the principle of unity, the principle of leadership? - Just like that. [[Abg. Dr. Rasinger: They're excluded!]] [[Abg. Schwarzenberger: If someone else has a different opinion, he is excluded from the party!]] On the occasion of this change of law and decision-making of the Medical Law 1998, I would like to lose a few words about the Chamber as such, because the Chamber is constantly being questioned. [[Abg. Meisinger: Not the Chamber, the Forced Membership!]] It is always called into question by compulsory membership. I want some thoughts – and maybe you listen a little bit now, then you'll get a little extra education! – present here for consideration. I'll just get them to think, and if you still have any objections, tell me. First: One has the impression that even today democracy is seen only in the antagonism of individualities and the state. In particular, so-called free ideologies can often be traced back to the agreed denominator: here the citizens and there the state. But that's not the whole truth! The whole truth is – and we know this – that there is something else between the individual citizens as individualities and the sum of the citizens as a uniform state will, and ultimately there must be in the interests of the freedom of citizens. We simply cannot deny that there are also legitimate partial interests whose persecution and integration is a state task. Society can not only be understood and represented by the state, it must also be understood and represented according to its division of interests. We have known for a long time – the fundamental research of a Lorenz of Stone comes from the middle of the 19th century. century – that interest is a structural element of society. In contrast to the interests of modern society, they are among the strongest drivers of economic and social progress – an insight that we find formulated by both theorists of social democracy and those of Catholic social doctrine. There is not a popular interest, but different interests in society, even though that may be very difficult to understand from certain ideologies. Secondly, You know that in Austria, in addition to the private-law organisations for the representation of interests, we also have legal representations of interests, which are set up as self-administrative bodies. This means that they are set up by the State by virtue of the act of sovereignty, that they are heard ex-legislatively that their institutions are appointed from the centre of self-government, that they are in principle financed from the resources of self-government members, that they have delegated competence to carry out public tasks and that they have power of authority that they are relatively independent, free of discretion to the State, but bound to State supervision. One must be aware of the difference between interest groups through private associations: Private associations are based on freedom of association, self-government bodies are based on the state's organizational power. Private associations are a free creation of citizens, self-government bodies are a creation of the state. The professional and economic self-management therefore serves, on the one hand, to carry out certain public tasks and on the other, the comprehensive representation of interests, the balance of interests, taking into account as far as possible all relevant individual interests. [[Abg. Gaugg: Who wrote you that?]] It thus fulfils the task and realization of the common good in a pluralist society. However, self-management requires – in itself a matter of course – also compulsory membership. The specific functions of municipalities, chambers and other self-government bodies shall not be performed by associations based on voluntary membership. Of course, this too can be thought of here... ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e8172	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to deal – like my previous speakers – with § 54, with the obligation to advertise and its new regulation. I think that she is now better regulated in this novel than before, and I would just like to underline all the comments made by my predecessor Mr Reitsamer. I would also like to point out that in the opinions of the federal states, some are concerned about the financial implications for the countries. I do not think these fears are justified, because according to the youth welfare statistics, 27 231 youth welfare measures were arranged in 1996, of which 2 503 were due to physical or sexual abuse. It should not be assumed that a sustainable increase in these figures is expected due to the current re-regulation of medical confidentiality, notification and reporting requirements. Even now, because of the provisions in force, the youth welfare sector and therefore the countries in cases of physical or sexual violence against minors are being dealt with. Under the new regulation, it is expected that the understanding of youth welfare providers will take place immediately and directly, not only after prior notification to the safety authority. This leads to a welcome acceleration of the steps to be taken. However, the new regulation does not mean an increase in the tasks of youth welfare providers, but merely that the tasks of the Youth Welfare Act are better - i.e. faster - to be carried out. Finally, it should be borne in mind that, in the number of 2 503 measures mentioned in 1996 because of physical and sexual abuses by minors, only a statistically unmarked part of the intervention will go down to medical intervention. In the case of a distribution of cases between about 130 youth welfare providers, therefore, there is only a scale of tasks which is not relevant to the discussion. It must also be mentioned that Austrian pediatricians and pediatric surgeons vehemently express themselves for the new medical law novelle. They welcome the newly regulated obligation of secrecy for doctors who put the aid to the children in the foreground. The Austrian specialist companies for child medicine and child surgery are also behind these new regulations. Professor Fasching and Professor Stögmann found to the APA only these days that, because of the current legal situation in many doctors, there is great uncertainty as to in which cases to show and in which cases not. This legal uncertainty can now be eliminated. With the revision of the 1994 Criminal Code, it became possible to waive the notice of the criminal law if this serves to protect the relationship of trust. Today, the medical law of this regulation is to be adapted. This can also help families as a whole, because an advertisement and criminal proceedings endanger a family in crisis still further. It must be assumed that someone who abuses a child is sick himself and therefore needs help and treatment. The mandatory advertisement, however, lowers the perpetrator's willingness to undergo treatment, and relief measures for the families would not be possible. It is not enough to order treatment, but also the willingness to do so is undoubtedly a prerequisite for success. The fact is that most of the cases of child abuse occur in the family. As a result of the mandatory advertising obligation, the abused children were burdened by a number of administrative activities which they continued to remain in a victim role. There, the new regime will be able to intervene and better match the interest of victims in direct assistance, in the treatment of physical and mental injury and in protection against further damage in many cases. We are not satisfied with the arrest of the perpetrators, but we want to help. I believe that this novel can contribute to this. We will therefore be happy to give this novelle our approval.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-10-08-020-XX-NRSITZ-00142_d7e8484	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! In response to a request from the National Council's Presidential Conference, I have agreed to inform the National Council today about the so far caused by the Leoben Public Prosecutor's Office in connection with Lassing's pit accident. However, I would like to ask you to understand that I am not going to disclose any details in terms of content, to make no assessments of the results so far, or to draw any quick conclusions. With regard to the non-publicity of the criminal procedure, to criminal-tactic considerations and to the interests of data protection, the Federal Minister for Justice, as an administrative body, is limited in public statements on pending criminal proceedings, and I have to impose on myself those restraints which I also expect from the authorities that I have assumed. Ladies and gentlemen! In connection with the mine accident of Lassing, the prosecutor's office is currently investigating Leoben against unknown perpetrators because of the suspicion of negligent common risk under § 177(1) and (2) criminal code. So far, the subject of the investigations has been two main themes: First, the question of the cause of the mine collapse on 17 November. In the light of the above, the Court of First Instance held that the Court of First Instance had failed to fulfil its obligations under the law of the Court of First Instance. Secondly, the mountain complex, including the question of a criminal responsibility for the ten people who were later shed in the given danger situation on the evening of the 17th century. ==References====External links==* Official website From the outset, Leoben's prosecutor's office was trying to launch the investigations necessary for the rapid and comprehensive investigation of the facts. She's still on Saturday, 18th. In the morning of July 1998, the Criminal Department of the State Army Command for Styria asked to include immediately the surveys necessary to clarify the situation on the spot. At the same time, the Landesgericht Leoben was asked to draw an expert from the field of mountain damage from the judicial preliminary surveys and to order the unsaided finding and subsequent advice. ==References====External links== In July 1998, Univ.-Prof. Dr. E. C. appointed an expert. ==References== In July 1998, the German Univ.-Professor Dr. Hollmann was appointed as an expert in the place of the German Univ.-Professor Dr. Hollmann. In addition, the German geologist Dipl.-Ing. Dr. Meissner appointed as an expert for the assessment of soil, mountain and hydraulic questions. ==References== In July 1998, the Landesendarmeriekommando was commissioned to buy documents from Naintsch Mineralwerke GmbH. ==References== In the afternoon of July 1998, a local suction permit was issued in Lassing, where the investigating judge, the expert, officials of the State Army Command and the prosecutor took part. The local eye-catching certificate was also used for short informative discussions with the head of operations and the head of operations as well as, in agreement with the management of operations and operations, for the acquisition of documents, mainly plans. After the nightly production of copies by the National Army Command, the originals were returned in accordance with agreement in the early days of the next day. As a result, the investigations were carried out, in particular by the Criminal Department of the National Armed Forces Command, under the participation of the two experts appointed by the Court. Between the Leoben judicial authorities and the investigating security service, discussions are ongoing on the state of the art of the investigations. In this context, taking into account the results of the expert's successive findings, further necessary and targeted action will be discussed. After the judicial system was able to take possession of the relevant plans and documents very quickly, the expert Dr. Hollmann soon realized that the overrun of the mining horizon is likely to have been at least co-existent for the pit accident. The further investigations carried out by the public prosecutor and the security authorities are also based on this knowledge. It may also be considered as assured that in this area, authorizations have been removed beyond that granted. However, the criminal issues of causality have not yet been resolved. A final assessment of the damage can only be carried out after the written opinion has been delivered. In any case, however, test drilling may be necessary. According to the survey, experts are confident that they will be able to deliver an opinion this year. The closer circumstances associated with the distribution of another ten people and the attempts at their recovery are still being worked out. If it has proved to be reliable, how the areas of responsibility are stored, who at what time had the knowledge and the like, the suspects will be made concrete. The safety investigation procedure is already so advanced that a report in the form of a summary report is likely to be expected in October 1998. Then, against those persons who have come up with a concrete suspicion of action according to the results of the investigation, they will be able to begin the investigation. It is only then, if it is established which persons are to be heard as suspects and who have a witness role, that precise demarcation of the obligations of truth is possible, as is necessary for further criminal proceedings. Ladies and gentlemen! To date, the judicial system has been able to address the consequences of this accident unimpeded and unimpeded by external influences. According to the Procuratorate Leoben, there was no intervention or intervention by superior departments or by other authorities or ministries in the interests of physical processing. The intervention by the Federal Minister for Justice, Dr. Farnleitner, in a newspaper interview concerned the intervention of the Minister of Justice. In the morning, I was asked by telephone to me whether it would be useful, in view of the possible unimpeded rescue efforts, to draw the manager off from his participation in the recovery tests at that time for the purpose of an expected multi-hour hearing. I have essentially answered this question to the effect that the time of the hearing should be assessed from a criminal point of view, which I saw to be out of order, since at the moment this could only be done by the security authority responsible for the investigations and also by the hearings. As you know, the hearing took account of the ongoing attempts to save the inaccurate has actually taken place only at a later date. Ladies and gentlemen! The Procuratorate Leoben has also stated that there is no evidence that any attempts have been made on the part of the Bergbehörde or the plant management to eliminate or suppress evidence or documents. Rather, within a very short time, it was possible to obtain and view all the documents described by the expert for the investigation as relevant. Therefore, there has been no need for criminal procedural measures so far. There would be no evidence at present to suggest that blackouts would have been made. There are no grounds for arrest in anyone who is a hypothetical suspect. Thus, the fact that there is still an investigation against unknown perpetrators does not fear any impairment of the criminal proceedings. At present, there is no evidence of the intentional crime of persons whose decisions could be linked to the disaster in Lassing. Furthermore, the sub-summation under § 177 of the Criminal Code is only a first legal assessment on the basis of the previous state of knowledge and does not in any way include further surveys on the question of the degree of indebtedness. Ladies and gentlemen! I can assure you that justice will continue to work objectively, objectively and without delays, and it can be assumed that there will be a clarification of the serious circumstances of this so terrible and humanly tragic accident. - As far as my education is concerned.

It is because it is a matter of resolving a conflict as quickly as possible, which actually brings an entire profession into discredit in the population. I have no understanding of measures – although the concerns may well be justified – that motivates children to demonstrations by threatening them to simply delete the favorite events, winter or summer sports weeks. The basic problem has already been identified in many debates today. It was shown that there are various and impenetrable salary calculations. Mrs Petrovic, I give you a hundred percent right: One must be a skilled man to see how the salaries are actually calculated. I can therefore be quite logical and logical to follow the demands of Mr Antoni, who speaks of a common basic teacher training. Our current system already produces the inequalities in the so-called "teaching market" – under quotation marks. There is a shortage on one side – and on the other side surplus. There is a lack of trained social education, but a surplus, for example, of primary school teachers in certain subjects, and it is not difficult to locate why it is so. One teacher has a lot of responsible and very difficult work to do and gets relatively little paid for. There is a lack of teachers for polytechnic schools, in the area where young people are to be prepared for entry into the profession, and there is a surplus of teachers for higher education in general. This joint training must, logically, result in a common law on service and salary. It has already been said today that everything is to be recorded in a law indicates the obligations of a teacher in his teaching activity. Although I do not wish to condemn this § 61 in general, I would also like to welcome it because it contains a lot of fair approaches, I would say that I believe that it is a disadvantage for some teachers who teach more than just in class, and I would like to present it on the basis of three examples. There are teachers in volunteer fire brigades. This is neither insignificant nor necessary in rural areas, because these teachers are tangible in the field of employment. If they have the wrong to provide assistance and have to stand for the neighbor, according to § 61, a financial withdrawal of permanent multiservices or a repetition of the missed teaching time is the result. We have similar things at the Jugendrotkreuz. This institution lives for school and school. In order for teachers to carry out their tasks, they need appropriate training in first aid, in swimming lessons a training for lifeguards, and then they have to pass on these skills – and do so, they have financial disadvantages. - That's how I could continue this list. But one thing seems to me to be still important: Due to educational considerations, we have repeatedly called for a location-related teacher training. The teachers now submit to her on school autonomous days, they have financial losses due to the follow-up arrangements. I asked you, Mrs Minister, at Question Time in June, whether derogations are possible for these teachers who work in the field of charity. You didn't. If one were to recognize that this service provided for the benefit of society, the community, was a educational link, then these teachers would not be adversely affected. Above all, this section 61 contains the fact that those teachers who do only classes in front of the class and nothing else associated with much extra work are rewarded, and those who prepare for the students, organize project weeks, projects or put themselves in the service of the general public, as shown by my examples, are punished by financial deductions, by follow-up hours or by the non-disbursement of supply hours. We have a system in which I have a pedagogical disability, and that, Mrs Minister, it is soon to be eliminated. Finally, I would like to say the following: I cannot identify with these statements made by some economic operators in the public, namely that teachers receive not only the quarter free of charge at project weeks, but also the sports equipment provided, in addition to having the lift card paid and in the end taking away their own family for free holidays. We must guard against such statements by some economic operators, and I myself as a teacher reject these accusations vehemently.

I now come to the Unemployment Insurance Act. There are two major changes to the right to emergency aid: the extension of the ten-year period within which eight years of employment must lie at the time of the reference of karenz money and part-time aid. This is particularly important for women in whom care is paid because of births. These are hardly capable of fulfilling these eight years of employment in recent years and will not be able to provide emergency aid. This hardness has been eliminated. The entry into force of this scheme and at the same time the four new criteria for the right to emergency aid were met. ==References====External links== The four new criteria for the right to emergency aid – eight years of employment in ten years, the fulfilment of compulsory schooling in Austria, the birth of Austria, or half a lifetime in Austria – should initially not be applied until 1 January 1997. In January 2000 the Commission adopted a proposal for a Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to turnover taxes. According to the Constitutional Court of Justice, restrictions based on citizenship criteria or on holders of an exemption are of the 11th century. It is therefore no longer possible to apply constitutionally and therefore no longer. This entry into force of the new criteria for all cases starting from 1. April 1998 is now bringing about equality for emergency recruiters. The AMS has also been informed that, in view of this amendment, as Mrs Minister has told us, it is no longer necessary to adopt decisions on the basis of the old legal situation. It has therefore come to the removal of discrimination due to citizenship, which also confirms to us Mr Kier. But he says in his dissenting opinion that there is now discrimination due to the privilege of birth. The Federal Chancellor's Office's Constitutional Service calls these new criteria constitutional, non-equivalent and EU-compliant. According to the Constitutional Court, emergency aid can also be differentiated according to objective criteria. Such an objective criterion undoubtedly represents integration in Austria. This integration in Austria is assessed according to the duration of employment, the school visit half in Austria or the duration of the stay. Birth is also an objective criterion for integration. If not before the eyes of Mr Kier, this novelle, I believe, will exist before the Constitutional Court. A few words on Mr Madl's request concerning the practical limitation of secondary incomes in the case of insurance cover: It is obvious from her that an improvement has already been made : [[Intrusion of the Abg. Madl]] In the case of karenz money, the net income from temporary employment is increased to 50 percent after deduction of the low-quality limit. [[New call of the abg. Madl.]] The monthly net income from temporary employment shall be equal to half the amount of the benefit due during the calendar month, in so far as it exceeds the respective monthly margin of low-quality. The remaining entitlement shall be deducted from the days of employment on which income has exceeded the daily limit. They require a conversion period of 18 months and provide an example of a holiday representation with an income of 30 000 S. I consider the first one to be a bit unrealistic, and second, our current and decisively better regulation aims to achieve better compatibility with childcare – that is the purpose of the karenz money – and to achieve more continuity in employment, which should improve re-entry opportunities. In our view, therefore, there is no need for action here.

On the comments of Mr Kier: He feared that certain provisions in the field of emergency aid could be unconstitutional. I do not share these fears, because I think that the current regulation is socially acceptable and also constitutionally in order. We used to have a different arrangement. We have primarily seen emergency aid as a provision until the relevant judgment of the European Court of Human Rights. By the way, this opinion was also shared by the Constitutional Court at the time. It was only by changing the jurisdiction of the Court of Human Rights that we had to make a change in this area. In doing so, we have adopted a new scheme, which is also socially acceptable and which is more focused on the nature of insurance. I therefore think that this regulation will be maintained. Moreover, in recent years, we have not guaranteed any change in the social field in respect of the Constitutional Court by constitutional provisions, even though this might have been quite tempting in the context of budgetary reorganisation. We have not done so out of respect for the Constitutional Court. But the most important thing in the field of emergency aid is not just that we have good social provisions. In our case, the emergency aid – unlike other states – is still not limited in time when it is granted. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: It doesn't have to be so great!]] But that's not enough. The most important thing is that we strive – and this is done by the Social Ministry, by the Labour Market Service, by our entire economic policy – to try to ensure that unemployed people find employment. In particular, we have special programmes for long-term unemployed people, which must be developed even further. In 1996 we started by changing the law so that emergency aid can also be given in such a way that the unemployed person, who is entitled to emergency aid, receives it as a wage grant and with the additional remuneration he may receive from a non-profit institution, receives at least a collective contractual income. At the same time, of course, he has much better chances of finding a job in the economy. This area should be further expanded, with the labour market service of this public sector as its own business area. It is also important that the labour market service in this area, where emergency aid is available without any additional burden on the budget for active labour market policies, is given much more tasks and interests, for example with the municipalities and so on. There is a lot of work that is important for the general public, but for which the entire money is missing to the respective organizations. But with the emergency aid as a wage grant, this can be implemented. One of the areas, for example, would be to make Austria even more disabled, with its own company organization. In this way, we could create new jobs through communities and other interested parties who have money, but not so much that they actually create jobs, along with emergency aid, to avoid unemployment, to create new jobs, and thus to fulfil a social task that respects – even if it is not set down by law – that everyone who wants work should also have the right to work and not just to emergency aid. Thank you.

This means, on the one hand, that it acquires retirement insurance periods, but on the other hand it is also insured in the event of a disease. That is why I would also like to thank the Federal Minister and her team for the support that has led to the amendment of this law.
